2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 上周三美國公布 8 月民間就業人數僅增加 3.8 萬人，低於市場預期的 4.8 萬人。
教育與醫療服務業新增 4.5 萬人，但製造業減少 1.7 萬人，專業與商業服務業
也出現萎縮 。 這反映出民間部門招聘動能放緩 ， 尤其是製造業與專業服務業的疲
弱。美元指數至高點回落 ， 惟上周五美國公布8 月非農就業人數新增 16.2 萬人 ，
遠超預期的 5.6 萬人 ，失業率維持在4.1%；勞動參與率回升至 61.6%， 平均工時
延長至 34.4 小時 ， 顯示勞動市場的韌性 。 雖然薪資增幅僅3.1%， 通膨壓力有限 ，
但強勁的就業數據仍強化了市場對美聯儲加息的預期 。 芝商所 FedWatch 工具顯
示，9 月加息機率已升至 62%，美元指數止跌回穩。
⚫ 紐西蘭央行上週三將官方隔夜拆款利率調升 25bps 至 2.75%， 並釋出緩步緊縮訊
號。央行聲明指出，逐步撤回貨幣政策刺激有助於通膨回到 2%目標中點，同時

降低未來大幅升息的必要性 。 總裁佈雷曼強調 ， 升息時機仍高度不確定 ， 短期核
心通膨可能續升 ， 但逐步退出寬鬆政策仍屬合宜 。 央行亦提醒經濟存在顯著下行
風險，政策將保持漸；然而消息公布後紐幣大跌，主因 2027 年利率預測較原先
預期低。
⚫ 加拿大央行上週三維持政策利率在 2.25%不變 ， 但警告通膨風險升高 。 美國新關
稅增加經濟不確定性 ， 或使企業延後投資與招聘 。 總裁麥克勒姆表示 ， 關稅對整
體經濟影響有限 ， 但中東戰事與高油價可能推升物價壓力 。 央行強調需警惕外部
因素對通膨的擴散效應，加拿大幣止跌回穩。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
美元兌新台幣匯率走勢
Sep/7/2026
＜上週走勢＞
上週台幣匯價高點為 31.590，低點在 31.827，以 31.630 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 0.2 分。
上週台幣先貶後升，週初，聯準會主席華許於全球央行年會發表的鷹派言論強
化市場對聯準會升息的預期，引發台灣市場股匯雙殺，外資匯出令台幣走貶；週二
早盤由於台股走升外資匯入，台幣一度走升，惟逢低進口商買美金力道強勁，加以
投信亦匯出，台幣由升轉貶，不過逢高出口商拋匯，台幣貶幅收斂；週三、周四，
中東戰事升溫，帶動美元指數走升，外資連續兩日賣超台股共計1390 億，匯出力道
強勁，台幣貶至周內低點 31.827，惟出口商拋匯需求及央行進場調節，抑制台幣貶
值空間；臨近周末，受益於美元指數走跌、亞幣走升，台幣止跌回穩，加以外資轉
向匯入，台幣收復上周大部分跌幅，終場在央行調節之下收在 31.630。
＜未來分析＞
預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國勞動力市場尚屬穩健，非農就

業人數遠超預期，勞動參與率回升，令市場對於聯準會升息的預期有所回溫，將給
予美元指數支撐；就台灣市場來看，台灣 8 月製造業 PMI 54.7，顯示台灣製造業景
氣續呈大幅擴張，新增訂單持續暢旺，有利台幣表現，惟上週外資賣超台股並於外
匯市場匯出，將為台幣帶來貶值的壓力。綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢區間震盪。
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.500-31.850

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Sep/7/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週人民幣震盪走升。週初，儘管華許鷹派言論提振美元指數，然而中國出口
商逢匯價反彈結售美金，帶動人民幣震盪走升，惟人民幣中間價升值速度放緩，顯
示監管單位在地緣政治風險再起的情況下，允許人民幣升值的意願有所降低，抑制
人民幣升值速度；周間由於人民幣已走升至年內高點附近，升值空間有限，日內人
民幣整體維持高檔窄幅盤整的格局 ， 週三晚間美國公布ADP 就業人數低於預期 ， 美
元指數走弱，人民幣受益走升；臨近周末人民幣開出中間價格為 6.7787，升破 6.78
整數大關並創三年半新高，加以日幣延續升值的態勢，同為亞幣的人民幣亦持續走
升，終場境外人民幣收在 6.7048，週線升值 264 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.7108，週
線升值 161 點。  
＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣區間偏升。國際市場方面，美國非農就業人數遠高預期，顯示
美國勞動力市場仍具韌性，加以美伊地緣政治風險近來有升溫的現象，油價上彈再
度引發通膨升溫的擔憂，市場仍持續定價聯準會九月升息的可能性，美元指數有望
獲得支撐。中國國內方面，人行中間價格接連兩日創近三年新高，釋放人民幣偏強
信號， 支撐人民幣延續升值的方向 。 技術面方面，USD/CNH 匯價居於各天期均線下
方，且均線向下發散，加以匯價有效跌破 6.7150 關鍵支撐位，空方動能較盛，顯示
人民幣將延續升值的態勢。綜合以上，預估本週人民幣走勢偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6800-6.7300

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/

美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Sep/7/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 USD/JPY 先上後下。週初，因美伊不確定性再次升溫，油價上漲推升通膨
預期，美元自近期的低點反彈，帶領 USD/JPY 上升到 160 上方；週間，市場對日本
官方可能採取支撐匯率措施的預期升溫，且傳出日本央行可能在近期會議上宣布升
息及加快正常化步伐，帶動日圓連續兩天大幅走升；臨近週末，美元指數止穩於年
線附近，USD/JPY 跌深反彈，終場收在 156.24，週線大跌 381 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間偏空震盪，主要原因如下：一、日圓上週飆升，數據證
實非官方干預，主因日銀審議委員高田創釋出鷹派信號，市場押注 9 月升息機率達
75%，疊加聯準會理事沃勒釋放按兵不動傾向 ，美日利差有望縮窄推升日圓。二 、美
國 8 月非農新增就業達 16.2 萬人遠超預期，帶動9 月升息機率上升至 62%，並強化
年底前升息兩次之押注。儘管川普發出中斷貿易威脅強推降息，但在就業穩健與主
席華許偏鷹派立場下，決策焦點全面轉向本週即將公布 CPI 數據，美日利差及油價
壓力預計將限縮 USD/JPY 下行空間。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 在日
央加速升息的預期下急跌，然匯價於 155 整數關卡止跌，若能有效跌破則可能還有
一波較大的跌幅。綜合以上，預估USD/JPY 本週呈現區間偏空震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：154.00-158.00 
前週交易區間 
155.27 160.39

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/

美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
         Sep/7/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 EUR/USD 震盪走升。週初，歐元跌深反彈，不過在美伊不確定性再次升
溫及美國利率維持偏高路徑的定價支撐美元需求下，EUR/USD 弱勢整理；週間，市
場在等待美國就業相關數據公布，歐元持續走落，最低來到 1.1565；臨近週末，美
國就業數據好於預期，但聯準會官員鴿派言論，加上川普發言使升息預期受抑制，
帶動歐元先下後上，終場收在 1.1613，週線上漲 33 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、美國 8 月非農就業增長超
預期，帶動交易員對聯準會本月重啟升息的押注顯著升溫。對此，美國總統川普上
週五強烈抨擊高利率使美國陷入劣勢，揚言若聯準會不調降借貸成本，美國將直接
停止與對美享有貿易順差的國家進行貿易 ， 意圖以極端貿易禁令迫使央行寬鬆。二、
受能源推動，歐元區 8 月通膨升至 3.3%，但核心通膨降至 2.4%，顯示油價上漲未
出現進一步滲透至工資、終端服務定價等長期通膨預期的二輪效應。歐洲央行管委
科赫示警通膨上行風險，強化 9 月升息預期，短線上為歐元提供支撐。三、技術面
而言 ， 在日線圖中 ，EUR/USD 匯價在多次向下測試 1.1550 的關鍵價位均獲得支撐 ，
目前承壓於年線但獲得月線支撐。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現區間震盪的
可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1540-1.1680 
前週交易區間 
1.1565 1.1641

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                         新台幣利率走勢分析

115.08.31-115.09.04 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.66% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15106   1.90% 
－ 5 年期- A15108   1.82%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，本週邁入新的提存期，因應 9 月有升息預期及季節性用
款因素 ， 銀行間調度偏保守 ，惟整體市場資金供需仍屬平穩， 令短率持穩於近期
區間。 
週四央行標售 364 天期定存單 1200 億元 ， 加權平均得標利率為1.643%， 符合
市場預期區間 1.64%-1.66%，較上期標售結果上升 1.7 個基本點。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.66%~1.68%，利率大致持平於上週。 
二、 本週市場交投清淡，5 年期指標券 A15108 週二搓合成交在 1.82%，10 年期指
標券 A15106 則未成交，最近成交利率為 1.90%。 
週二標售 5 年期公債得標利率 1.82%， 落在市場預期區間1.80%~1.82%上緣 ，
投標倍數 1.57 倍為 2024 年 11 月以來最低 ， 顯示在國際債市氣氛偏空影響下 ，
市場投標態度謹慎。 
韓國、日本央行處於升息循環階段、聯準會主席 Warsh 暗示若 PCE 未往 2%
目標邁進將有升息可能 ， 在台灣經濟成長與通膨維持高位下 ， 台灣央行面臨緊
縮壓力 ， 加上美伊戰事再度升溫 ， 美債殖利率將呈震盪偏升格局 ， 預期台債殖
利率將隨美債走勢維持高檔盤整，預估 5 年券區間為 1.80%~1.85%，10 年券
區間為 1.90%至 1.95%。

## Document 062: 2026年9月宏观数据点评：9月宏观数据总体改善，三季度经济增长动能回升

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年9月宏观数据点评：9月宏观数据总体改善，三季度经济增长动能回升.pdf
Report source: 东方金诚
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

东方金诚宏观研究

1 
9 月宏观数据总体改善，三季度经济增长动能回升 
——2026 年 9 月宏观数据点评 
首席宏观分析师 王青  研究发展部执行总监 冯琳 
事件：据国家统计局2026 年10 月19 日公布的数据，2026 年三季度GDP 同比增速为4.5%，二季度为
4.3%，前三季度 GDP 增速为 4.6%，去年全年 GDP 增速为 5.0%；9 月规模以上工业增加值同比实际增速为
5.5%，8 月为5.2%，1-9 月为5.3%，去年全年为5.9%；9 月社会消费品零售总额同比增速为1.1%，8 月为
0.4%，1-9 月为 1.1%，去年全年为 3.7%；1-9 月全国固定资产投资累计同比下降 7.2%，前值为-7.2%，去
年全年为-3.8%。 
基本观点： 
总体上看，主要受出口增速大幅加快，消费增速改善及投资降幅收窄带动，三季度经济增速企稳回
升，新旧动能转换持续推进。9 月工业生产加速，主要受采矿业以及电力、燃气及水的生产和供应业增加
值增速回升拉动，当月制造业增加值增速受“中秋错期”扰动小幅放缓，但仍处较快增长水平。前三季
度，受内需走弱、部分行业限产影响，工业增加值增速较上年总体有所回落，但在出口 增速高位上行、
高技术产业增加值两位数增长带动下，三季度工业生产增速较二季度明显加快，是当季经速增速上行的
主要推动力量。消费方面，9 月社零增速加快，主要受去年同期基数走低，“中秋错期”对今年9 月社零
同比表现形成正向拉动，以及电子产品、石油制品等商品涨价影响。受上年同期基数下行等影响，三季
度社零增速较二季度有所上行，但当前消费偏弱局面仍有待改善。投资方面，1-9 月固定资产投资同比增
速为-7.2%，降幅与前值持平，三大投资板块增速为“两降一升”。其中，1-9 月基建投资同比降幅略有
扩大，但在稳增长政策发力支撑下，9 月当月投资降幅收窄；9 月8000 亿新型政策性金融工具开始投放，
1-9 月制造业投资同比降幅较前值收窄；最后，三季度房地产市场延续调整，1-9 月房地产投资同比降幅
扩大至-20.6%，是当前整体投资负增长的最大拖累因素。 
往后看，四季度出口仍然有望保持 20%以上的高增速，9 月末以来系列稳增长政策出台后，四季度投
资增速有望止跌回稳，楼市调整幅度将会收敛，低基数下居民消费增速也将有所回升。这将带动四季度
工业生产增速上行，服务业生产增速也有望加快。初步估计，四季度 GDP 增速将加快至 4.7%左右；全年
GDP 增速将在 4.6%附近，落在“4.5%至 5.0%”的目标范围之内。我们预计，着眼于稳定明年一季度宏观
经济运行，后期包括全面降息在内，总量型稳增长政策还有发力空间。

2 
   具体解读如下： 
一、三季度 GDP 增速企稳回升，背后主要是当季出口增速高位上行，外需对经济增长的拉动力增强，
同时，三季度商品消费增速也有所加快，投资降幅略有收窄。这带动当季工业生产显著加快，新旧动能
转换提速。在系列稳增长政策出台后，预计四季度 GDP 增速将进一步回升至 4.7%左右，完成全年经济增
长目标已没有悬念。 
三季度 GDP 增速达到 4.5%，较二季度加快 0.2 个百分点，呈现企稳回升势头。背后的主要推动因素
是三季度出口增速加快。以美元计价，当季出口同比增长28.4%，较二季度加快 8.3 个百分点，并带动当
季贸易顺差同比扩大 27.2%，较二季度上升 26.4 个百分点，意味着三季度净出口对经济增长的拉动力显
著增强。此外，三季度国内投资降幅有所收窄，居民商品消费增速改善，也对经济增长动能转强有一定
支撑作用。供给方面，三季度出口高增叠加 以高技术制造业为代表的新动能领域增长势头强劲， 共同带
动当季工业生产增速达到5.1%，较二季度加快0.4 个百分点，是当季GDP 增速加快的主要推动力量。 
三季度宏观经济的主要亮点在于，在 AI 投资热潮推动下，当季高技术制造业和现代服务业增速高位
上行，新旧动能转换加 快，整体就业市场保持稳定 ，物价水平温和回升。数据显示，三季度代表整体经
济物价水平的 GDP 平减指数同比为 1.5%，连续两个季度保持正增长。这意味着此前的物价低迷局面得以
扭转，有助于宏观经济走出“物价负增长——经济增长动能减弱”的负向循环。 
往后看，四季度出口仍然有望保持 20%以上的高增速，9 月末以来系列稳增长政策出台后，四季度投
资增速有望止跌回稳，楼市调整幅度将会收敛，低基数下居民消费增速也将有所回升。这将带动四季度
工业生产增速上行，服务业生产增速也有望加快。初步估计，四季度 GDP 增速将加快至 4.7%左右；全年
GDP 增速将在 4.6%附近，落在“4.5%至 5.0%”的目标范围之内。我们预计，着眼于稳定明年一季度宏观
经济运行，后期包括全面降息在内，总量型稳增长政策还有发力空间。 
二、9 月工业生产加速，主要受采矿业以及电力、燃气及水的生产和供应业增加值增速回升拉动，当
月制造业增加值增速受“中秋错期”扰动小幅放缓，但仍处较快增长水平。前三季度，受内需走弱、部
分行业限产影响，工业增加值增速较上年总体有所回落，但在出口 增速高位上行、高技术产业增加值两
位数增长带动下，三季度工业生产增速较二季度明显加快。 
9 月规上工业增加值同比增长5.5%，增速较上月加快0.3 个百分点，工业生产总体延续回升态势。分
门类看， 9 月采矿业增加值同比增长 1.3%，增速较上月回升 2.7 个百分点，是当月工业生产增速加快的
主要拉动项。采矿业生产增速回正，主要受两方面因素带动：一是煤矿复产稳步推进，9 月规上原煤产量
同比下降 6.8%，降幅较上月收窄 0.9 个百分点；二是原油生产提速，9 月规上原油产量同比增长 2.2%，

3 
增速较上月加快 1.8 个百分点。9 月电力、燃气及水的生产和供应业增加值同比增速也有所加快，从上月
的 4.9%回升至5.6%，主要与去年同期基数走低有关。 
作为工业最大门类，9 月制造业增加值同比增长6.0%，增速较上月小幅回落0.1 个百分点，但仍处较
快增长区间。当月制造业生产较快增长，主要受三方面支撑：一是出口保持高增，9 月规上工业企业出口
交货值同比增长 17.0%，增速较上月加快 5.9 个百分点，对生产的拉动进一步增强。二是高技术制造业延
续两位数增长，当月增加值同比增长 15.3%，前值 16.7%。三是国内稳增长政策发力，带动投资、消费下
行压力边际缓和。需要说明的是，9 月制造业增加值增速小幅回落主要受中秋假期错期扰动——今年中秋
落在 9 月、去年在10 月，导致今年9 月工作日比上年同期少1 天，并不意味着制造业景气走弱。 
累计看，1-9 月规上工业增加值同比增长5.3%，增速较上年全年放缓0.6 个百分点，较上半年小幅放
缓0.1 个百分点。今年以来工业生产增速有所回落，一方面受内需走弱拖累，另一方面“反内卷”持续推
进，煤炭等行业限产，也对工业生产形成一定下拉。不过，与需求端投资、消费相比，今年以来工业生
产仍维持较快增长，宏观经济“供强需弱”特征较为明显。这背后有两大支撑： 
一是出口持续高增，外需对工业生产的拉动作用不断增强。1-9 月规上工业企业出口交货值同比增长
10.7%，增速较上年全年加快 8.5 个百分点，较上半年加快 1.0 个百分点。二是在市场供需两旺、财政金
融政策支持力度持续加大的有利环境下，1-9 月高技术产业增加值同比增长 14.3%，增速较上年全年加快
4.9 个百分点，其中，三季度同比增速为16.3%,二季度为 14.0%。目前高技术制造业在规模以上工业生产
中的占比在17%左右，能够对整体工业生产形成较为强劲的拉动作用。 
值得一提的是，三季度工业生产较二季度有所加快，是推动 GDP 增速上行的主要推动力量。数据显
示，当季工业增加值同比增速为 5.1%，较二季度加快 0.4 个百分点，主要受出口大幅走强以及高技术产
业生产加速拉动。 
展望四季度，出口有望保持高增，国内稳增长政策发力将带动投资改善， 居民消费增速也将有所回
升，叠加高技术制造业生产将延续较快增长，以及去年同期基数走低，预计工业生产增速总体上将较三
季度进一步加快。考虑到“中秋错期”对工业生产的扰动将在 10 月消退，当月工业增加值增速有望加快
至 5.7%左右。 
三、9 月社零增速加快，主要受去年同期基数走低，“中秋错期”对今年9 月社零同比表现形成正向
拉动，以及电子产品、石油制品等商品涨价影响； 受上年同期基数下行等影响，三季度社零增速较二季
度略有上行，但当前消费偏弱 局面仍有待改善。 背后是受补贴政策退坡、房地产市场持续 调整、居民消

费信心偏弱等因素叠加影响 ，同时，二季度以来 城镇居民人均可支配收入中位数同比增速 放缓，也是近
期消费增速仍然偏低的重要原因。 
9 月社零同比增长1.1%，增速较上月加快0.7 个百分点，分项中，商品零售和餐饮收入同比分别增长
0.9%和 1.2%，增速较上月分别加快 1.6 和 0.6 个百分点。当月社零增速回升，背后主要是去年同期基数
走低，以及“中秋错期”对今年9 月社零同比表现形成正向拉动。同时，从环比看，9 月社零季调环比增
长 0.35%，明显好于上月的下降 0.16%，显示消费增长动能边际改善，但仍低于过去十年同期 0.83%的平
均增长水平，消费偏弱局面仍有待逆转。 
从限额以上商品零售额看，汽车销售下滑继续对社零整体增速产生较大拖累。受新能源车购置税免
税政策退坡、前期以旧换新政策透支居民购车需求等因素影响，9 月汽车零售额同比下降 21.2%，降幅较
上月扩大2.7 个百分点。剔除汽车后，9 月除汽车以外的消费品零售额同比增长3.9%，明显高于当月社零
整体增速。 
从其他商品来看，9 月限额以上各类别商品零售额增速普遍加快，主要受基数走低及“中秋错期”影
响。值得一提的是，尽管去年同期基数有所走高，9 月通讯器材零售额同比增速仍较上月加快3.8 个百分
点至 31.1%，石油及制品零售额同比降幅较上月收窄 6.2 个百分点至-0.8%，这主要受相关商品价格上涨
拉动。不过，9 月家电零售额同比增速较上月放缓 0.4 个百分点至 1.9%，金银珠宝、体育娱乐用品、家
具、建筑装潢材料零售额延续负增长，尽管降幅与上月相比有不同程度收窄，这主要受补贴政策拉动效
果减弱、房地产市场持续下行、居民消费信心偏弱等因素叠加影响。 
累计看，1-9 月社零同比增长 1.1%，增速较上年全年放缓 2.6 个百分点，较 1-6 月放缓 0.2 个百分
点。从当季表现看，三季度社零同比增长 0.7%，较二季度 0.2%的增速有所反弹，主要是受上年同期基数
下行影响。同时，1-9 月服务零售额累计同比增长5.0%，增速较上年全年回落0.5 个百分点，较1-6 月回
落 0.3 个百分点。需要指出的是，受“中秋错期”影响，9 月当月服务零售额同比增长约5.8%，增速较8
月大幅加快1.6 个百分点。 
今年以来，尤其是二季度以来，社零表现走弱，服务消费增速亦有所走低，主要与补贴政策退坡、
房地产市场延续下行等因素有关。其中，房地产市场下行是主要拖累，这不仅直接抑制涉房消费，也在
持续影响居民消费信心。数据显示，8 月消费者信心指数录得89.5，仍明显低于100 的中值水平。同时值
得关注的是，今年 1-9 月城镇居民人均可支配收入累计同比增长 4.5%，增速较一季度和上半年分别加快
0.3 和 0.1 个百分点，但人均可支配收入中位数同比增速较一季度和上半年分别放缓0.5 和 0.1 个百分点

至 3.9%。收入均值与中位数增速背离，表明整体收入改善主要受人工智能及有色金属等少数行业居民收
入大幅上行带动，其他多数中低收入群体收入改善相对滞后。这也是二季度以来消费增速总体放缓的重
要原因。 
展望后续，接下来财政金融协同促消费政策有望进一步加力，各地财政部门会出台更多促消费“自
选动作”，国内促消费政策力度有望加大，加之上年同期低基数影响，四季度社零同比有望加速。我们
也认为，四季度加发5500 亿地方政府债券，其中3000 亿用于提高县区一般公共预算保障能力，也有助于
推动四季度消费增速加快。其中，10 月“中秋错期”因素拉动作用退去，社零增速有可能会小幅放缓，
但仍有望保持在1.0%左右，而在低基数带动下，11 月和12 月社零同比增速将加速至2.0%附近。 
四、投资：1-9 月固定资产投资同比增速为-7.2%，降幅与前值持平，三大投资板块增速为“两降一
升”。其中，1-9 月基建投资同比降幅略有扩大，但在稳增长政策发力支撑下，9 月当月投资降幅收窄；
9 月 8000 亿新型政策性金融工具开始投放，1-9 月制造业投资同比降幅较前值收窄；最后，三季度房地
产市场延续调整，1-9 月房地产投资同比降幅扩大至-20.6%，是当前整体投资负增长的最大拖累因素。 
分季度来看，三季度固定资产投资同比增速为 -10.4%，降幅略小于二季度的-11.1%。数据显示，三
季度三大投资板块中，除房地产投资降幅扩大外，基建投资、制造业投资降幅均较二季度有所收窄。 
基建投资方面，1-9 月投资增速为-4.2%，降幅较前值扩大 0.2 个百分点；据我们测算，三季度基建
投资同比下降 7.9%，二季度下降 8.2%。其中，9 月当月基建投资同比下降 5.4%，降幅较上月收窄 1.5 个
百分点。我们认为，9 月当月基建投资降幅收窄，背后的主要推动因素包括：9 月国家发改委等部门加力
推动“六张网”等重点项目建设；当月地方政府专项债发行 6200 亿，为年内最高水平，其中用于化债的
资金规模为 1800 亿，占比明显下降，用于项目建设的资金规模为4400 亿，创年内最高——这或表明，9
月化债对项目资金的挤占效应显著减弱，基建投资动能增强；最后，9 月 8000 亿元新型政策性金融工具
启动投放，也对当月基建投资形成较强拉动作用。 
往后看，伴随9 月末央行宣布降低PSL 利率，将“六张网”建设纳入支持范围，加之四季度财政支出
和各类政府债券资金使用赶进度，特别是 8000 亿新型政策性金融工具在四季度全面推进，以及加发的
5500 亿地方政府债券中将有2500 亿用于项目建设，这些都将推动“六张网”和重大项目建设进度显著加
快。我们判断，年底前基建投资增速有望由负转正。这是接下来“加大逆周期调节力度”的主要体现，
也会成为推动今年投资“止跌回稳”的主要拉动因素。 
制造业投资方面，1-9 月累计同比增速为-2.1%，降幅较前值收窄 0.2 个百分点。据我们测算，三季
度制造业投资同比下降 3.8%，二季度为同比下降 3.9%。其中，9 月制造业投资同比下降 0.9%，降幅较 8

月显著收窄 5.2 个百分点，主要原因是当月稳投资政策发力，特别是8000 亿新型政策性金融工具开始投
放，其中部分资金用于芯片制造等先进制造业投资。另外，上年同期投资基数大幅下行，也会推动三季
度制造业投资同比数据改善。需要指出的是，受政策倾斜叠加市场供需两旺驱动，当前高技术产业投资
处于较快正增长状态，1-9 月同比增速达到4.9%。其中，航空、航天器及设备制造业，电子及通信设备制
造业投资分别增长12.5%、7.6%。 
往看后，着眼于推动投资止跌回稳，接下来 8000 亿新型政策性金融工具、今年超长期特别国债等支
持的制造业项目投资会加快推进，会对四季度制造业投资有支撑作用。我们判断，1-10 月制造业投资下
行势头会进一步收窄，年底前月度制造业投资同比增速有望回正。 
房地产投资方面，1-9 月累计同比增速为-20.6%，降幅较前值扩大 0.7 个百分点。据我们测算，三季
度房地产投资同比下降26.4%，二季度同比下降23.0%，其中，9 月房地产投资同比下降26.3%，降幅较8
月扩大0.8 个百分点。近期房地产投资下滑幅度扩大，背后是三季度以来房地产市场延续调整。其中，三
季度新房、二手房销售增速都有所下行，70 城二手房房价指数环比跌幅也略有扩大。另外，当前金融机
构对房企融资仍然偏于谨慎。 
9 月 29 日，全国范围内针对刚需购房实施定向贴息的政策出台，预计将对楼市有一定提振作用。不
过，8 月 28 日公布系列房地产新政，主要内容包括推动商品房现售，完善房地产开发贷管理，延长居民
按揭贷期限等，核心是将 “房住不炒”制度化，加快构建房地产行业发展新模式。这有可能在短期内加
大房企资金压力，接下来房地产投资还将较大幅度下行。我们预计，1-10 月房地产投资同比降幅有可能
扩大至21.0%左右。总体上看，年底前房地产投资降幅收窄的难度很大。

元富證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市敦化南路二段 97 號 19 樓 (02)2325-3299  (1 15)金管投顧新字第 016 號 
公司拜訪快報

南亞科(2408) 
建議：維持買進 
結論：2027 年產業供需吃緊不變，長期 AI 營運占比挑
戰五成

一、南亞科為利基型 DRAM 製造廠，2026 年資本支出
上修至 697 億元(YoY+420%) 
南亞科為利基型 DRAM 製造商 ， 最大股東為台塑集團之
南亞塑膠持有 29.28%股權，主要競爭對手包含
Samsung、SK Hynix、Micron 以及華邦電，目前月產能
為 6.5 萬片，20nm 和 1B 為主力生產製程。依產品終端
應用區分，消費性電子和 AI 分別占營收比重為 60%+和
20%+。公司維持 2026 年銷售量年增 15-20%目標，因
5A 新廠需待 2H27 量產且存貨處於低水位 ， 故銷售量年
增率不如去年亮眼。至於在資本支出方面，2026 年資本
支出由 520 億元上修至 697 億元，其中五成將用於生產
設備 。 此外 ， 新廠將於1Q27 開始裝機 ，2H27 開始量產，
2028 年達成產能 30K， 新廠全產能由45K 上修至 50K，
投入資金為 4800 億元。 
二、客戶新 LTA 生效致使 ASP 季增幅超過六成，3Q26 
25.4 元續創新高 
3Q26 營收 1336 億元(+62%QoQ)，受惠於 LTA 長約價
格調漲，ASP 季增超過六十位數百分比，銷售量和匯率
則分別為季減中個位數和季增低個位數 ， 受惠於ASP 大
幅上揚且折舊費用持穩 ， 毛利率85.7 優於前季之 79.5%，
營業利益 1068.9 億元(+76%QoQ)，稅後淨利 876.2 億
元(+75%QoQ)，EPS 25.4 元續創新高。 
三、DRAM 供不應求延續，但 ASP 增幅將收斂，估計
4Q26 EPS 28.13 元 
DRAM 產業供不應求持續 ， 原廠供給持續向AI 傾斜 ， 由
於合約價已大幅調漲，ASP 增幅將收斂，估計 4Q26 營
收 1474.1 億元(+10.3%QoQ)，毛利率改善至 86.6%，
營業利益 1196.5 億元(+12%QoQ)，稅後淨利 970.4 億
元(+11%QoQ)，EPS 28.13 元。  
四、原廠新產能規劃搭配客戶長約需求，且 HBM 投片
占比增加，2027 年產業供不應求已成定局  
在產業需求方面，全球九大 CSP 2027 年 Capex 1.38
兆美元(+50%YoY)，AI 運算將主導 DRAM 需求成長，
帶動 DRAM 需求從 HBM、LPDDR5 擴展至網路 、 儲存，
至於傳統應用則面臨供給不足以及價格調漲，導致產品
降規或生命周期延長，部分低階市場需求則需仰賴回收
DRAM。在產業供給方面，雖供應商整體擴充產能，但
產能規劃將搭配客戶長約需求，有助長期供需穩定，加
計 HBM 於產品投片占比由今年 22%增加至明年之 30%，
致使 DRAM 新增產能主要用於 HBM 生產。故以整體市
況而言，AI 強勁需求將推動記憶體供需結構改變，降低
景氣循環對於供應商影響，產業庫存仍偏低，2027 年產
業供不應求可望延續。 
在 DRAM 報價走勢上 ， 雖消費端壓力未減 ， 但AI Server
增量需求支撐 4Q26 記憶體合約價漲勢 ， 集邦預估4Q26
傳統型 DRAM 價格季增 10-15%低於前季之 13-18%，
就南亞科而言 ， 營收占比達50%+的 LTA 客戶已於 3Q26
大幅調漲價格，故預期 4Q26 價格漲勢將趨緩。 
五、新廠產出效益於 2H27 開始發酵，長期 AI 產能占比
上看五成 
南亞科今年資本支出從 520 億元上修至 697 億元，主因
為生產設備投資增加，5A 新廠維持 1Q27 裝機規劃，
2028 年和 2030 年投片目標分別為 30K 和 50K， 整體新
廠投資規模達 4800 億元 ， 由於5A 新廠量產預計於 2H27，
故 4Q26-2Q27 出貨量約當 3Q26，至於屏東 3D 晶圓封
裝測試廠預期在 2028 年底前導入矽穿孔(TSV)和晶圓堆
疊等先進技術。此外，儘管資本支出積極，目前AI 基礎
設施(包含 Server、網路、儲存以及系統管理)客戶營收
占比超過 20%， 但新產能擴建主要以AI 客戶需求為主，
故經營階層預期 2029-2030 年 AI 營收占比將上看
40-50%。 
六、維持買進理由 
1)預估 2026 年和 2027 年 EPS 分別為 75.63 元和
119.26 元。 
2)考量 2027 年產業供需吃緊不變，長期 AI 營運占比挑
戰五成，維持買進評等，TP 660 元 (2027 PBR x 2.8)。

展望未來，南亞科(2408.TT) 因受限於極低庫存，2027 年上半年
位元出貨量預期將保持平穩，待 4Q27 5A 新廠開出產能後，出貨
量將恢復逐步成長；產能方面，5A 新廠規劃於 2027 年第一季開
始進場裝機，第一階段預計 2028 年底前投片量達 3 萬片，全產
能 5 萬片目標於 2030 年底達成，且擴建嚴格以客戶 LTA 的 AI
專案為導向；此外，公司規劃於屏東科學園區建置3D 晶圓封裝與
測試廠，預計 2028 下半年導入設備，並與客戶合作開發客製化  
AI/WoW(晶圓堆疊)與 ASIC 專案，已開始產生部分營收貢獻，長
期目標於 2029 至 2030 年將 AI 基礎設施營收占比拉高至 40%
至 50%；技術面上，次世代 1C 製程預計於 2027 年第一季進行
客戶驗證 。 綜合以上 ， 研究部預估南亞科2027 年 EPS 為 130.72
元。考量目前公司記憶體供給吃緊、需求擴大，有長期合約支持未
來成長 ，且公司具備獨特製程升級與客製化產品線成長潛力 ， 研究
部給予 5.5 倍本益比評價，目標價 710 元，投資建議為買進。

⚫ 公司基本資料 南亞科(2408.TT)公司簡介 
南亞科技(2408.TT)成立於 1995 年，隸屬於台塑集團，為全球領先的動
態隨機存取記憶體(DRAM)製造大廠 。 公司生產基地主要位於台灣 ， 擁有
多樣化的產品組合，涵蓋 DDR5、LPDDR5/5X、DDR4、LPDDR4/4X、
DDR3 及 LPDDR3 等產品線 ， 產品種類達數十顆 ， 服務全球數百家客戶 。
面對傳統記憶體產業之劇烈循環波動，公司近年積極推動技術升級與結
構性轉型，除中長期積極投入 1B(16 奈米)與 1C(12 奈米)先進製程轉換
及新五廠建置，並深入布局企業級 SSD(eSSD)、SmartNIC、BMC 等
AI 伺服器基礎設施，同時與客戶合作開發客製化 AI/WoW(晶圓堆疊)利
基產品，正逐步由傳統大宗商品化DRAM 廠，轉型為具備高附加價值與
高敏捷度的全方位利基型記憶體解決方案供應商 。

2Q26 營運表現全面成長，售價調漲驅動獲利大幅提升 
南 亞 科 (2408.TT) 2Q26 營 業 收 入 為 825.49 億元 (Q+68.17% / 
Y+684.23%) ， 營收展現強勁成長 ， 受惠於平均銷售單價(ASP)季增超過
60%的漲勢，有效抵消了位元出貨量與上季持平的量能限制；而在 ASP 
大幅上漲且成本結構維持穩定的推升下，單季毛利率提升至
79.49%(Q+11.60ppt / Y+100.05ppt) 。雖然員工分紅與銷售研發使營業

3Q26 獲利大幅跳升，低價長約重簽驅動 ASP 季增逾六成

然出貨量因公司整體庫存已降至極低水位，呈中個位數季減，但整體營

幅 上 漲 且 生 產 成 本 結 構 穩 定 的 推 升 下 ， 單 季 毛 利 率 跳 升 至

為 75.88 億元 。 綜合以上 ， 南亞科(2408.TT) 3Q26 營業收入為 1,336.49
億元， Q+61.90%/Y+611.70% 、 毛 利 率  85.70% ，

展望 2026 年全年，南亞科(2408.TT) 前三季累計淨利已高達 1,639 億

位數百分比的目標；合約與定價機制上，公司目前長約(LTA)覆蓋率落在 
50% 至 70% 之間，其中 3 年期以上長約占總出貨量 20% 以上，履
約率高達 100%，預期第四季 DRAM 供需緊缺狀態延續，合約平均售
價保持溫和成長；需求端方面，GPU、TPU、CPU 及 ASIC 等 AI 基
礎建設建置強勁，推升 AI 伺服器與基礎設施相關產品之營收占比突破 
20%； 產品組合中 DDR4 與 LPDDR4 合計營收占比超過 60%，DDR5 
占比約 10%。管理層指引目前產業短期內難以緩解供給壓力，隨著第四
季 DRAM ASP 持續看漲 ， 公司毛利率表現有機會再進一步拉升 。 綜合以
上，研究部預估南亞科(2408.TT) 2026 年營業收入為 4,190.95 億元，
Y+529.40%、毛利率  82.61%，Y+60.10ppt、營業利益率  76.83%，
Y+68.96ppt、稅後純益 2,642.16 億元，Y+3,894.93%、EPS 為 76.58
元。

長鑫存儲(CXMT)技術進展與產業影響 
長鑫存儲(CXMT)於 2026 年 9 月宣佈第五代 DRAM 制程(G5)進入量產階
段。儘管目前 G5 產品仍處於客戶驗證與良率提升階段，距離大規模商
業化出貨尚需時間 ， 但已顯示中國DRAM 技術發展速度明顯超出市場原
先預期。 長鑫 HBM3E 良率已達 40%左右。市場普遍認為，長鑫在無法
取得 EUV 設備的情況下，主要透過 ArF DUV 浸潤式曝光搭配多重曝光
(Multiple Patterning) 技術推進制程微縮，因此雖然能夠達到接近國際大
廠先進 DRAM 節點的設計能力 ， 但製造成本 、 良率與生產效率仍與三星 、
SK 海力士及美光存在差距。未來能否持續提升至國際大廠 70%以上的
量產水準，將是觀察長鑫HBM 競爭力的重要指標。短期而言，長鑫技術
進步對台廠商影響有限，長鑫目前仍面臨良率提升與客戶驗證所需時
間，短期內難以快速增加有效供給，因此全球 DRAM 供應緊張的局面仍
將延續至 2027 至 2028 年。相較之下，三星、SK 海力士與美光持續將
產能轉向 HBM 產品，反而造成 DDR4 供給逐漸退出市場，進一步支撐
DDR4 價格維持強勢，有利於以 DDR4 產品為主的南亞科營運表現；然
而，中長期則需注意，長鑫 DRAM 投片量將於 2027 年底達到約 30K 
WPM，並持續擴產，若未來 G5 良率持續改善、HBM 技術逐步成熟，
加上中國市場本土化政策支持，長鑫未來在中低階 DDR5、PC DRAM
及中國本土市場的競爭力可能快速提升 ， 進而對全球DRAM 供需格局產
生實質影響。

長鑫存儲(CXMT)4F²技術 
長鑫存儲在 10 月 12 日有表示年底推出 4F²架構 DDR5 伺服器記憶體，
4F²是將每個記憶體儲存單元配置在面積相當於 4 倍 F 平方的區域內 ， 其
中 F 代表半導體制程的最小線寬，這種設計能讓同一顆晶片容納更多儲
存單元，進而提高記憶體容量。目前，主流商用 DRAM 仍採用 6F 平方
儲存單元結構。長鑫存儲表示，其4F 平方設計的核心，在於自主研發的
垂直環繞閘極(Vertical Surrounding Gate，VSG)電晶體，這項技術透過
垂直閘極架構改善漏電問題，同時大幅縮小單元面積，提高儲存密度。
SK 海力士(SK Hynix) 已於 2025 年公佈 4F 平方技術發展藍圖，另外，
根據南韓媒體今年 4 月的報導，三星電子(Samsung Electronics)計畫於
2028 年前，量產採用這項新設計、制程低於 10 奈米的 DRAM。

結論與建議 
展望未來 ， 南亞科(2408.TT) 因受限於極低庫存 ，2027 年上半年位元出
貨量預期將保持平穩 ， 待4Q27 5A 新廠開出產能後 ， 出貨量將恢復逐步
成長；產能方面，5A 新廠規劃於 2027 年第一季開始進場裝機，第一

### Page 4

第一金證券投資顧問股份有限公司(First Capital Management Inc.) 台北市長安東路一段 35 號 3F   TEL(02) 2563 -1666

4
階段預計 2028 年底前投片量達 3 萬片 ， 全產能5 萬片目標於 2030 年
底達成，且擴建嚴格以客戶 LTA 的 AI 專案為導向；此外，公司規劃於
屏東科學園區建置  3D 晶圓封裝與測試廠，預計 2028 下半年導入設
備，並與客戶合作開發客製化 AI/WoW(晶圓堆疊)與 ASIC 專案，已開
始產生部分營收貢獻，長期目標於 2029 至 2030 年將 AI 基礎設施營
收占比拉高至 40%至 50%； 技術面上 ， 次世代 1C 製程預計於 2027 年
第一季進行客戶驗證。綜合以上，研究部預估南亞科(2408.TT) 2027 年
營業收入為 7,103.14 億元，Y+69.49%、毛利率 84.00%，Y+1.39ppt、
營業利益率 78.64%，Y+1.81ppt、 稅後純益 4,510.28 億元 ，Y+70.70%、

EPS 為 130.72 元。考量目前公司記憶體供給吃緊、需求擴大，有長期
合約支持未來成長，且公司具備獨特製程升級與客製化產品線成長潛
力，依據美光27 年本益比區間約 5 至 6.6 倍，研究部給予5.5 倍本益比
評價，目標價 710 元，投資建議為買進。

 歐美航線運價優於預期，預估 3Q26 稅後 EPS 19.62 元：3Q26 營收
1,467.76 億元(+51.4%Y oY)，優於預期。受惠運價上漲獲利率提升，
然考量燃油成本占整體營業成本逾 2 成，油價上漲仍對成本形成壓力，
預估稅後淨利 424.74 億元(+95.3%Y oY)，稅後 EPS 19.62 元。

望後市，根據 Clarksons Aug -26 預測，2026 年全球貨櫃供給年增
4.6%，需求年增 3.8%，供給增幅仍高於需求，惟供需缺口較 2025 年
逐步收斂。預估 2026 年營收 4,735.4 億元(+24.9%Y oY)，稅後淨利
991.6 億元(+44.6%Y oY)，稅後 EPS 45.8 元。展望 2027 年，隨新船
陸續交付，儘管塞港情況仍可能限縮有效 運力，惟考量整體供給成長
仍高於需求，預期市場運價將逐步回歸常態。整體而言，預估2027 年

稅後 EPS 22.28 元。

長榮 為 全 球 貨 櫃 運 力 第七大 航
商，市占率 5.9% 
 長榮成立於 1968 年 9 月，實收資本額 216.5 億元。截至 3Q26，全球超過 1.3
萬位員工及 293 個代理行，航線擴及 101 個國家，總船隊數共 242 艘(自有船
比例超過 6 成)，預計至 2030 年仍有 72 艘船待交付。根據 Alphaliner 統計，
截至目前長榮為全球貨櫃運力第七大航商，市佔率約 5.9%。長榮 1H26 營收
結構為貨運收入 93%，船艙租收入 1.2%，其他 5.8%。

率 105.5%，優於預期，主要受惠歐美航線運價走升，且旺季附加費及燃油附
加費挹注營收 。獲利方面 ，受惠運價上漲帶動獲利率提升 ， 惟中東衝突於Jul-26
一度趨緩後再度升溫，且考量燃油成本占整體營業成本逾 2 成，油價上漲仍對
成本形成壓力，預估 3Q26 毛利率 38.91%。由於營收規模擴大，費用率有望
下降 ，預估營益率 35.71%，假設 匯率 持穩 ，預估稅後淨利 424.74 億元
(+164.9%QoQ，+95.3%Y oY)，稅後 EPS 19.62 元。

(+0.05%WoW，+215.76%Y oY)，隨中國十一長假結束，歐線運價短期反彈，
分別為遠東-歐洲線+3.41%WoW，遠東-地中海線+0.2%WoW；美線持平，遠
東-美西線+0.08%WoW，遠東-美東-0.04%WoW。觀察 10/11 公告跨大西洋、
歐 洲 及 跨 太 平 洋 航 線 運 力 分 別 變 動 -1.14%WoW 、 -1.88%WoW 及
+0.48%WoW。整體而言，隨歐美航線逐步進入傳統淡季，航商陸續調整運力
配置，其中歐洲線運力投放減少，美線則大致持平，顯示航商持續透過運力調
節支撐市場運價。

展望 4Q26，需求面而言，歐線隨傳統旺季結束，終端需求動能轉弱，預期後
續運價將呈緩步回落走勢。美線方面，旺季需求已近尾聲，且部分船班裝載率
已有下滑，儘管 Sep-26 川習會後美國調降部分消費品關稅，包括玩具、小型
家電及節慶裝飾品等，惟目前尚未出現明顯大型備貨潮。供給面而言，隨水情
陸續改善 ， 巴拿馬運河管理局預計自2026/10/15 起將每日允許通行量由 32 艘
回升至 33 艘，惟仍低於原先 36 艘水準。考量大型貨櫃船通行具一定優先性，
目前對船期營運影響相對有限，惟通行量尚未完全恢復，仍可能使美東線有效
運力有所收斂。另，美伊衝突及紅海通行風險仍存， Gemini 聯盟持續恢復部
分紅海航線，近期亦傳出 Premier Alliance 有望於 4Q26 恢復部分紅海航線，
若後續其他航商陸續跟進，繞航所吸收之有效運力恐逐步釋放，對市場運價形
成壓力。

整體而言，儘管近洋線進入傳統旺季，惟長榮歐美航線營收占比較高，預期整
體運價及貨量將隨淡季到來而回落 ， 預估4Q26 營收 1,350.87 億元(-8%QoQ，
+57.6%Y oY)。獲利方面，儘管油價仍維持高位，惟隨貨量減少，相關變動成
本亦有望下降，預估毛利率 37.9%。另考量長榮往年於 4Q 提列年終獎金費
用，預期費用率將季增，預估營益率 29.4%。假設匯率持穩，預估稅後淨利
323.53 億元(-23.8%QoQ，+279.8%Y oY)，4Q26 稅後 EPS 14.94 元。

2026 年有效運力供給受限 ， 預估
稅後 EPS 45.75 元 
 展望後市，根據 Clarksons Aug-26 預測，2026 年全球貨櫃供給年增 4.6%，需
求年增 3.8%，供給增幅仍高於需求，惟供需缺口較 2025 年逐步收斂。短期而
言，受到缺工及天候因素影響，部分港口作業效率下降，近期塞港現象增加，
有助吸收部分有效運力。展望 2027 年，在尚未考慮紅海全面復航之前提下，
Clarksons 預估全球貨櫃供給+8.8%Y oY，需求+3.5%YoY，供需缺口將再度擴
大；若紅海航線進一步恢復，繞航所吸收之有效運力亦將逐步釋放，供給壓力
恐進一步增加。需求方面，則須持續關注停戰協議進展、美國關稅政策變化及
通膨壓力對終端需求之影響。

展望 2026 年，長榮持續優化航線布局，強化東南亞、中東、非洲及中南美洲
等區域配置 ， 並透過長約布局降低市場運價波動影響，整體而言 ，元富預估 2026
年營收 4,735.4 億元(+24.9%Y oY)。獲利方面，預估毛利率 30.8%，營益率
25.4%，稅後淨利 991.6 億元(+44.6%Y oY)，預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 45.8 元。
展望 2027 年，隨新船陸續交付，全球貨櫃船隊運力持續增加，儘管塞港情況
仍可能限縮有效運力，惟考量整體供給成長仍高於需求，預期市場運價將逐步
回歸常態。整體而言，預估 2027 年營收 4,097.3 億元(-13.5%Y oY)，稅後淨利
482.4 億元(-51.4%Y oY)，稅後 EPS 22.28 元。

Update
Largan Precision  | Asia Pacific
3Q26 Results Miss on Lower
GPM, but FAU/CPO
Development Well on Track
Largan reported 3Q26 net income of NT$5.182bn, down 27% YoY.
The company reported a 11% YoY revenue decline, to NT$15.651bn; gross margin
declined YoY and QoQ to 42.2% in 3Q26, lower than expected due to lower new-
project yields and structural green electricity cost hikes.
Management expects shipments to be flat MoM in October and decline

sequentially in November as order momentum is weaker-than-expected.
FAU/CPO development well on track: First FA  project passed customer
verification; expanding from FA into FAU, including coupling and potential FAU-to-
carrier assembly, with a dedicated new facility expected to support mass production
from around mid-2027. We maintain our long-term positive view on Largan's FAU/
CPO opportunities.

Morgan Stanley Research 3
Valuation Methodology and Risks
Largan Precision (3008.TW)
Base case: Our price target is based on an SOTP valuation, comprising the traditional business
and potential FAU opportunity. We value the traditional business using a residual income
model, consistent with our Greater China Hardware Tech coverage, assuming an 8.5% cost of
equity, a 7% net profit CAGR over 2026-36E and a 3% terminal growth rate. We separately

value the FAU business, assuming a 40% market share in 2028.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected high-end smartphone demand
n Faster-than-expected penetration of periscopes
n Stronger-than-expected CPO volume growth
Risks to Downside
n Greater-than-expected competition, which would erode Largan's ASP and gross margin
n Weaker-than-expected smartphone demand, especially for high-end models
n Market share gains and more pricing pressure from Sunny

n Weaker-than-expected CPO volume growth

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

주가 추이

견조한 판매 확대로 매출 견조, 이익은 시장 기대치 하회 예상 
3Q26 동사 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 33.6조원(+17.3% 이하 Y oY)과 2.39조
원(+63.6%) 기록 전망. 3Q 매출액은 친환경차 중심 신차 확대 통해 두 자릿수 
성장세를 기록. 3Q 판매량은 도매 기준 84.3만대(+7.6% 이하 Y oY)로 전년 동
기 대비 성장 폭을 확대했고, 특히 친환경차 판매량 29.8만대(+45.9%)를 기록
하며 이번 분기 매출 성장에 절대적으로 기여.  
다만, 3Q 수익성은 4~5월 상승했던 원자재 투입 영향과 ‘경제형EV 믹스’가 더
욱 확대(13.2% → 14.5%)되면서 단기 수익성에 부정적 작용하며 시장 기대치 
2.5조원을 다소 하회할 전망. 그러나, 기말 환율이 1,356원/$ 수준으로 낮 아 지
면서 전년 동기 수 익 성 악화에 크게 영 향 을 미쳤던 판매보증비 외화환산손익 
영향이 개선(1.6%p 수준) 기대되며, 관세 부담 또한 북미 HEV 확대로 전년 동
기 대비 약 4,500억원(2.0%p) 감소하면서 부정적 수익성 영향 만회 전망.  
유럽은 점유율 확대, 북미는 수익성 개선으로 
올해 북미 HEV와 유럽 BEV 중심 신차 출시 및 물량 확대가 본격화되면
서, ’27년에는 이익 성장 기여도가 확대될 것으로 전망. 미국 조 지 아 텔 루 라 이
드 HEV 신규 생산(2.8만대) 및 HMGMA 스 포 티 지HEV신규 생산(1.0만
대) 등 수 요 가 높은 북미 현 지 에 서 HEV 물량 확대 중으로 4Q에도 텔루라이
드 HEV와 스포티지 HEV의 현지 생산/판매 확대를 비롯하여 셀토스 HEV 
출시로 ’27년 북미 중심 HEV 신차 물량 증가 및 믹스 개선 기대. 유럽은 강한 
BEV 수요가 지속되면서 중국 업체들의 공세 강화 중이나 동사의 물량 확대 또
한 견조. 3Q EV3 2.9만대, EV5 2.2만대 등 유럽 BEV 수요 대응 전략 주효. ’27
년에는 K4/셀토스 및 쏘렌토 등 HEV 신차 중 심 의 친환경차 PT 믹스 다양화
로 유럽 내 물량 확대 흐름 지속 전망 
물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결, 기말 배당 매력도 부각 
’27년 동사 영업이익 추 정 치 는 11.1조원(+13.5%)으로 증익 지속 전망. 최근 
환율 추세 고려 시 내년 수익성에 부 정 적 영향 확대 가능성 존재하나, 올해 확
인된 신차 중심 물량 확대를 통해 부정적 환율 영향을 상쇄할 것 으 로 기대. 
12M EPS 추정치 하향(-2.9%) 및 상 반 기 적 용 한 코스피 프리미엄 제거(-
10%)로 목 표 주 가 를 260,000원으로 하향. 그러나, RA 설립에서의 동사 지분 
참여 및 기말 배당(’26년 DPS 7,000원 추정)에 따른 상 대 적 배당 수익 매력
도 부각 등은 주가 모 멘 텀 에 있어 기대 가능한 요소.

합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

## Document 241: Anthropic备战IPO：上半年净亏损超过807亿美元，总算力承诺支出将超6000亿美元

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic备战IPO：上半年净亏损超过807亿美元，总算力承诺支出将超6000亿美元.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

据《华尔街⽇报》援引 Anthropic IPO 招股说明书草稿报道，该公司总算⼒承诺⽀出将超过
6000 亿美元，未来⼗年在算⼒相关协议上的承诺⽀出合计逾 5180 亿美元，且正洽谈进⼀步
扩⼤计算资源。与此同时，该公司今年上半年营收达 163 亿美元，第三季度营收环⽐增⻓
45% ⾄ 168 亿美元。
摘要：
Anthropic 正以前所未有的速度烧钱扩张算⼒，同时加速推进其潜在的史上最⼤规模 IPO 。
10 ⽉ 20 ⽇，据《华尔街⽇报》援引 Anthropic IPO 招股说明书草稿报道，这家 AI 初创公司 2026 年
上半年净亏损超过 807 亿美元，其中 720 亿美元来⾃与可转换票据及其他融资⼯具估值变动相关
的会计计提，较去年同期 130 亿美元的净亏损⼤幅扩⼤。
与此同时，该公司今年上半年营收达 163 亿美元，第三季度营收环⽐增⻓ 45% ⾄ 168 亿美元。
Anthropic 在算⼒上的豪赌规模同样令市场瞩⽬。据华尔街⽇报报道，该公司总算⼒承诺⽀出将
超过 6000 亿美元，未来⼗年在算⼒相关协议上的承诺⽀出合计逾 5180 亿美元，且正洽谈进⼀步

扩⼤计算资源。
分析⼈⼠指出，上述⽀出体量对拟上市投资者⽽⾔是不容忽视的估值变量。 Anthropic ⽬前据报
道正寻求以 1.5 万亿⾄ 2 万亿美元估值于 11 ⽉挂牌上市。
算⼒承诺规模庞⼤，英伟达芯⽚需求激增
Anthropic 在计算基础设施上的投⼊规模正逼近极限。
据华尔街⽇报报道，该公司计划在算⼒⽅⾯的总开⽀最⾼可能达到 7500 亿美元，仅租赁英伟达
芯⽚⼀项就将⽀出最多 860 亿美元。2026 年上半年， Anthropic 在算⼒上的⽀出已约达 90 亿美
元， 2025 年全年的计算与基础设施⽀出为 73.3 亿美元。
据报道， Anthropic ⽬前正与英国新兴云计算提供商 Nscale 洽谈扩⼤算⼒合作，计划租⽤其位于
西弗吉尼亚州 Monarch 数据中⼼园区第⼆栋建筑的算⼒资源，新增约 460 兆⽡的计算容量，所采
⽤的是英伟达最新⼀代 Vera Rubin 芯⽚，并保留对规划中第三栋建筑的优先谈判权。
若该协议达成， Anthropic 与 Nscale 的总合同承诺价值将升⾄约 900 亿美元。

在云计算协议⽅⾯，据华尔街⽇报， Anthropic 将在⾕歌云协议上⽀出约 1110 亿美元，在亚⻢逊
AWS 协议上⽀出约 1100 亿美元，在微软云协议上⽀出约 310 亿美元。
营收⾼增⻓，但烧钱速度毫不⽰弱
尽管亏损数字触⽬惊⼼， Anthropic 的营收增⻓曲线同样陡峭。
据路透社报道，⼀位参加 Anthropic 在旧⾦⼭举⾏的上市前投资者会议的知情⼈⼠透露， 2025 年
全年营收增⻓ 12 倍⾄约 46 亿美元； 2026 年第三季度营收环⽐增⻓ 45% ⾄ 168 亿美元。
然⽽，⾼增⻓伴随的是持续的运营亏损。 2025 年，该公司运营亏损超过 80 亿美元（剔除与历史
融资相关的各类负债减记）。

## Document 243: August CPI – Came in on our expectations (2026-10-20)

Source PDF: all report pdf/August CPI – Came in on our expectations (2026-10-20).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 16
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #australia #rba #economicbulletinsaustralia #inflation
August CPI – Came in on our expectations
By Justin Smirk & Neha Sharma
15:27 September 30 2026 Share
August Monthly CPI: Headline 0.4%mth/4.0%yr, Trimmed Mean 0.2%mth/3.6%yr. Market
services ex volatile –0.4%mth/3.3%yr.

The CPI rose 0.4% in August, spot on Westpac’s expectation but a bit softer than market
expectations of 0.5%. Due to base effects the annual pace picked up to 4.0%yr from 3.5%yr.

### Page 2

Key factors driving the headline result were auto fuels (14.8%); games, toys & hobbies (10.2%) with
rents providing a solid base (0.3%) while some offset was provided by both domestic (–4.9%) and
international holiday travel & accommodation (–1.5%).
Trimmed mean (TM) inflation printed 0.2% again bang on Westpac’s expectation while the market
was slightly higher at 0.3%. The annual pace was flat at 3.6%yr, the third month in a row at this pace,
while the six-month annualise pace was steady at 3.9%yr.

Key factors driving the TM print were: rents (0.3%) and new dwelling purchases (0.2%). The TM
distribution ranged from –0.38% to 0.82%.
Today’s inflation update revealed price discounting for some durable goods as retail demand softens.
We would expect to see a bounce from these categories in the following month. There was also
offsetting strength elsewhere in durable good so at least for now, the momentum continues to
suggest some near term upside risk to our current inflation estimates.

Nevertheless, as the TM came in on expectations and based on a first quick pass at updating the
quarterly profile, we see little pressure to revise our current Q3 estimates of 1.3%qtr for the CPI and
0.9%qtr for the TM.
The RBA remains alert to near term inflationary risks. We are expecting the momentum in TM
inflation to remain well above the top of the target band through to year-end underpinning our
expectations for the RBA to raise rates again in November.

### Page 3

August CPI in more detail
Following the bumper read in July, the monthly CPI moderated in August rising 0.4%mth. This was in
line with our expectations and slightly softer than the market consensus. This took the year-ended pace
to 4.0%yr. On a seasonally adjusted basis the CPI rose 0.7%mth highlighting the impact of seasonal
sales in August.

They key drivers behind the monthly lift were automotive fuel, up 14.8%mth, contributing 0.5ppts to the
headline figure. The surge resulted from the ending of the fuel excise discount on August 3 and rising
global energy prices. With crude oil prices still elevated due to the conflict in the middle east, and
growing shortages of refined products due to both the middle east conflict and Ukrainian assaults on
Russian refining capacity, auto fuel is expected to remain an inflationary impulse at least until the end
of 2026.

The monthly TM also softened, rising 0.2%mth compared to the 0.5%mth read from July. The result
was again in line with our expectations, and very close at two decimal places (0.24%mth vs
0.23%mth), but softer than the market's view. The year-ended pace held at 3.6%yr for the third
consecutive month, although it continued to ease marginally at the second decimal place. In contrast,
the six-month annualised pace has continued to trend higher since April and is currently running at
3.9%yr.

### Page 4

There is a possibility that as the ABS continues to grapple with seasonal adjustment issues we will see
revisions to the TM estimates. Last July also saw a strong 0.48%mth read which eased to 0.29%mth in
August. This is not too dissimilar from the 0.48%mth and 0.24%mth reads for 2026.

We are expecting core inflation to start to moderate as we get to year end but our current estimate for
the month of December still has the TM running at a 3.3% in the year while the six-month annualised
pace is still a chunky 3.1%. Applying a trim on the seasonally adjusted 3m/3m expenditure classes
yields a TM of 0.9%qtr currently. This is in line with our current Q3 TM estimate.

Other measures of underlying inflation told a similar story, easing on a monthly basis but holding steady
on a year-ended basis. Market services ex volatiles fell –0.4%mth to be 3.3%yr higher. Excluding both
volatiles & holidays saw a 0.2%mth/4.1%yr lift.

Most market services components eased in the month (including on a seasonally adjusted basis) but
annual growth across this group as a whole remained above the RBA's 2-3% target band. The near-
term momentum (three-month annualised) is also quite strong across several components of services.
Of note include deposit and loan facilities (11.2%mth), sports participation (10.9%), clothing repair
(7.0%), restaurant meals (6.8%) and other household services (5.2%). We are closely watching market
services as this is where inflationary risks, outside of energy, appear to be most persistent.

Due to its sizable weight in the CPI, and the fact price changes here tend to be less volatile than many
other categories, so they almost always appear in the TM estimates, housing prices remain a key
focus. Rents rose 0.3%mth in August, in line with our expectations, to be up 3.6%yr. Since April, the
seasonally adjusted rents series has been running lower than the non-seasonally adjusted series. We
are expecting rents to hold around this pace till year end before moderating modestly as we move
through 2027.

New dwellings inflation was 0.2%mth in August (softer than our expectation for a 0.4%mth rise). Since
peaking in May, monthly price growth has slowed in each successive month, potentially reflecting
increasing difficulty for developers in passing through higher costs amid softer conditions in established
housing markets and weaker demand in the face of higher interest rates. The annual pace looks to
have topped out. It was at 5.8%yr in June easing to 5.4%yr currently with the three-month annualised
pace at 3.7%. We expect dwelling price inflation to continue easing through to the end of 2027 as
falling established house prices limit the ability of builders to pass on rising construction costs. There is
a medium-term risk that as conditions improve builders may raise prices more aggressively to claw
back margins.

### Page 5

Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated in August. The share of the basket running above 3%yr
eased from 45% to 42%, with the share below 2%yr increasing from 38% to 41%.

### Page 6

Key surprises in the month:
Upside surprises

Downside surprises
Insurance surprised to the upside (0.7%mth vs 0.3%mth) after a run of weaker-than-usual reads over
the past two months.
Motor vehicle repair costs rose a solid 0.8% against our 0.3%mth expectations, with spare parts and
accessories also up 0.3%mth vs –0.4%mth.
Footwear rose 1.7%mth against our expectations for a -2.9%mth decline, the gains were broad-
based across women, men & children.
Household goods surprised to the upside. Household appliances, utensils and tools rose 1.9%mth
against our flat expectations, meanwhile household textiles fell –1.0%mth against our expectations
for a –5.0%mth fall. Furniture and furnishings also fell by less than expected –0.6%mth vs –1.3%mth.
Recreation and culture surprised to the upside (–1.0%mth vs –1.5%mth), owing to a 1.4%mth lift in
other recreation, sport & culture against our expectations for a flat read on the back of an outsized
gain in games, toys & hobbies (10.2%mth vs 2.5%mth). Holiday travel was marginally less weak than
expected (–3.3% vs –3.5%mth).

### Page 7

Food was the key downside miss falling –0.1%mth against our expectations for a 0.2%mth lift. Meat
& seafood (–0.9%mth vs 0.5%mth) and fruit & vegetables (–1.2%mth vs 0.1%mth) were the key
areas of weakness.
Audio, visual and computing equipment and services came in weaker-than-expected (0.7%mth vs
1.2%mth), owing to a softer 0.6%mth lift in computing services (3.0%mth expected). This was partly
balanced by a stronger than expected lift in computing equipment (0.9%mth vs –0.5%mth). The latter

is where we are most likely to see the impact of the AI boom in the CPI, due to computer chip
inflation, other than construction cost inflation for new dwellings.
Motor vehicles came in lower at –0.4%mth vs –0.1%mth which could be the start of price pressures
associated with the rapid expansion of the share of Chinese cars in the Australian market.
Automotive fuel was also slightly softer at 14.8%mth vs 16.7%mth.
Garments and accessories were both softer. Garments fell –1.9%mth against our expectations for a

0.2%mth rise, meanwhile accessories came in at 0.1%mth vs 0.5%mth expected.

### Page 8

What does this mean for the RBA?
Yesterday, post the RBA rate hike and monetary policy decision statement, Westpac changed its view
from a November follow up rate hike being a risk to being the base case. The post-meeting statement
was notable for its emphasis on the Middle East conflict, noting that “global energy prices are now
much higher than had been assumed in the August forecasts”. In addition, RBA’s liaison suggests firms
are passing this on or planning to do so. The pass-through was characterised as partial, and additional

to domestically driven inflation arising from capacity pressures. We read this as implying that the RBA
is concerned about upside risks to underlying inflation in Q4 of this year, as well as Q3.

### Page 9

Related articles
Our current forecast of 0.9%qtr for the September quarter TM stands post today’s release and
represents a step up from 0.8% in the June quarter holding the annual pace at 3.6%yr. Our current
forecast for the Q4 TM is 0.7%qtr/3.3%yr but given the move in energy prices, and anecdotes of firms
now passing on those rising costs, we too see upside risks to this estimate.

As such, we hold to the view that the RBA will follow up with another rate hike in November. For more
information on the RBA decision please see Luci Ellis’s note “RBA hikes in September, November
follow-up now expected".

## Document 270: Consumer Checkpoint_ Strength in numbers September saw solid spending across many categories and a strong start to the holidays. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Consumer Checkpoint_ Strength in numbers September saw solid spending across many categories and a strong start to the holidays. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13026023     1

Economy 
Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers 
09 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Consumer spending remained very solid in September. Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), or 5.1% excluding gasoline, both the third strongest growth rates over the past four 
years. Month-over-month (MoM) spending was more muted, especially without gas. 
• Spending shows considerable breadth: the vast majority of discretionary retail and services categories are showing growth. 
Moreover, this strength extends across income cohorts, with the ongoing recovery of lower- and middle-income households' 
spending growth. 
• With the winter months fast approaching, holiday spending is already increasing at a solid rate, though it's still early. In Bank of 
America deposit data through September, buy now, pay later (BNPL) usage is rising but not at a rate that is overly concerning. 
However, nearly one in every five households that use BNPL has a repayment of over $500.

Consumer Checkpoint is a regular publication from Bank of America Institute. It aims to provide a holistic and real-time estimate of US 
consumers’ spending and their financial well-being, leveraging the depth and breadth of Bank of America proprietary data. Such data is not 
intended to be reflective or indicative of, and should not be relied upon as, the results of operations, financial conditions or performance of 
Bank of America. 
A solid September for spending   
Consumer spending continued to look solid in September. Bank of America total aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), while spending excluding gasoline increased 5.1% YoY. Notably, both are the third 
strongest growth rates in the past four years. Seasonally-adjusted total card spending rose 0.2% month-over-month (MoM), 
while spending excluding gasoline was little changed. Overall, spending growth remains well above the 2025 average rate 
(Exhibit 1). 
Exhibit 1: Total card spending per household rose 6.2% YoY and 5.1% YoY excluding gasoline in September

### Page 2

  I NSTI TUTE

Most spending categories are growing  
One striking thing about recent card spending, in our view, is that it is well balanced across categories. For example, both 
spending on retail (excluding gasoline) and services grew strongly YoY in September, at 3.5% and 5.4%, respectively. Meanwhile, 
retail spending is showing notably better momentum than in 2025 (Exhibit 2).  
Exhibit 2: Spending is rising across retail and services 
Retail (excluding gasoline) and services card spending per household (monthly, seasonally adjusted, index January 2025 = 100)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Well balanced across “nice-to-have” and “need-to-have” retail categories  
Is the momentum in retail spending reflective of “nice-to-have” discretionary or “need-to-have” necessity spending? In retail, 
this is a nuanced question, as many categories sit in a gray area between “necessity” and “discretionary”. General merchandise 
stores, for example, sell both essentials like groceries as well as nice-to-have electronics or even jewelry.  
In Exhibit 3, we account for this by grouping retail spending into “more discretionary” (furniture, electronics, building materials, 
online retailers, clothing retailers and miscellaneous stores), “partly discretionary” (auto, health and personal care retailers, 
general merchandise) and “mostly necessity” (gas and groceries). Even within these categories the edges will be blurred, but the 
overall impression is that retail spending growth is widely distributed, with spending rising across all fronts. One caveat: “mostly 
discretionary” spending has been making a smaller contribution since June. 
Exhibit 3: Retail spending is distributed across discretionary and necessity categories  
Percentage points contribution to YoY growth by type of retail spending (three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data. See methodology for category aggregation definitions.  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Looking at the share of “more discretionary” retail spending categories that are increasing YoY underscores that growth is well 
balanced and broad-based (Exhibit 4). Non-store and online retailers and miscellaneous stores saw some of the fastest growth, 
while furniture and building materials were among the laggards, though other durable categories showed some strength.

While some of this spending growth likely reflects the impact of higher prices pushing up spending across a swathe of

For services, growth is driven mostly by discretionary spending  
In services spending, on the other hand, the boundaries between discretionary and necessity spending are often clearer. Travel 
spending (e.g., airlines, lodging) can be thought of as, by and large, discretionary. At the same time, utility bills, rent and 
insurance payments are necessities.  
And in services, discretionary spending remains the clear driver, accounting for over three-quarters of YoY spending growth in 
September (Exhibit 6). Leisure and housing services are major components of growth, though travel and restaurants also made 
positive contributions. However, as we noted last month strength in airline spending largely reflects price rises rather than 
transactions growth (read more in Consumer Checkpoint: Still sizzling).  
Exhibit 6: Services spending growth has been mostly driven by discretionary spending over the past year

### Page 4

4 09 October 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

Lower-income households’ spending outpaced all but the top 5% 
The breadth in spending growth across categories is also evident across income cohorts. In September, lower-income 
households’ total card spending growth was 5.5% YoY, while the equivalent figures for middle- and higher-income households 
were 4.9% and 4.8% YoY, respectively. The top 5% of households by income continue to show the fastest spending growth, 
5.9% in September, though this has eased somewhat (Exhibit 7). 
Overall, the divergences across income cohorts are much smaller than they were in 2025 and the first three months of this year. 
Just focusing on spending excluding key necessities (gasoline, food and utilities) doesn’t meaningfully change the picture of 
convergence across income cohorts (Exhibit 8).  
Exhibit 7: The top 5% saw the strongest spending growth of any

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The strength in lower- and middle-income households’ discretionary spending is occurring despite necessity spending shifting 
from a tailwind to a headwind over the past year amid rising gas prices (Exhibit 9). In our view, a key component of why these 
households have been able to combine rising necessity spending with strength in discretionary is the labor market and a 
recovery in their after-tax wage growth (Exhibit 10).  
In September, lower-income wage growth eased to 4.5% YoY from 4.7% YoY in August, while growth for middle- and higher-
income households rose to 3.8% and 3.7% YoY, respectively. These changes were modest, and lower-income households are 
continuing to see faster after-tax wage growth than other cohorts, a notable turnaround from the “K-shaped” pattern we 
observed over much of 2025 and 2026.

Should we be concerned about the easing in household spending and after-tax wage growth for those with the top 5% of 
incomes? For now, probably not, as in our view this spending growth is likely being driven by wealth effects rather than wage 
gains. Absent a sharp correction in equity markets, there is little reason to expect them to retrench their spending. However, 
developments here are worth watching as this cohort comprises a significant proportion of overall consumer spending. They also 
tend to make outsized contributions to several discretionary spending categories (Exhibit 11).  
Exhibit 11: The top 5% of households by income make up a disproportionate amount of spending in highly discretionary categories

With summer gone, thoughts turn to the holidays and BNPL… 
Now that summer is over, the next big gauge of consumer momentum is how people spend during the run-up to, and over, the 
holiday season. It is early days, but September spending on holiday items ran well ahead of 2025 (Exhibit 12). Given the backdrop 
of firmer after-tax wage growth and breadth across retail and services categories, this is perhaps not surprising. However, some 
of this spending strength could be due to rising prices as opposed to more purchases.

A concern often raised as we move into the holidays is whether consumers will lean on less traditional forms of credit such as 
buy now, pay later (BNPL) to finance their spending. Frequent BNPL users tend to have higher credit card utilization rates (read 
more in: Do consumers have wiggle room to absorb higher gas prices?).  
Looking at the share of households in Bank of America deposit data with a BNPL payment, we do tend to see a seasonal rise 
over the holidays (Exhibit 13), though in past years, these increases have quickly reversed. Further, while the underlying trend in 
BNPL usage is upwards, there are no signs of a sharp acceleration. We do, however, see some evidence that BNPL is being used 
to finance more expensive purchases, with the share of households making repayments above $500 rising, albeit relatively slowly 
(Exhibit 14).  
Exhibit 13: 8.3% of Bank of America households had a BNPL 
payment in September

(monthly, $ amounts)

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The tendency towards greater usage is occurring across income cohorts, though is somewhat more pronounced for lower-
income households (Exhibit 15). However, higher-income households have the largest share of BNPL payments above $500 
(Exhibit 16). Overall, in our view, the increase in the share of BNPL users heading into the season does not appear too 
concerning, though the rate of usage for some BNPL consumers does bear watching.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Store category aggregation for “more discretionary”, “partly discretionary”, and “mostly necessity” is defined as follows: 
More discretionary: furniture, electronics, building materials, hobbies, miscellaneous stores, nonstore retail, clothing. 
Partly discretionary: auto, health and personal care, general merchandise. 
Mostly necessity: Gasoline, food and beverage.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts. 
The “more discretionary” retail category includes furniture, electronics, building materials, online marketplaces, clothing retailers and 
miscellaneous stores; while “partly discretionary” retail contains auto parts and services, health and personal care retailers, general merchandise 
and “mostly necessity”  retail contains gas and groceries. 
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

### Page 1

Taiwan Industrials

Investment case: We initiate coverage of Acter, an established 
multinational cleanroom engineering, procurement, construction and 
management (EPCM) turnkey service provider, with a Buy (1) rating. Its 
core business includes cleanroom electromechanical engineering, 
water/gas/chemical delivery systems, and other cleanroom services. We 
believe now is a good time to revisit the stock as it underperformed the 
TAIEX Index by 32.7% in 3Q26, mainly on weaker-than-expected 2Q26 
results and soft sector sentiment. Given its stronger revenue momentum 
over recent months and upcoming TSMC results on 15 October, which may 
drive sentiment, we suggest investors to buy the stock. We forecast a 
24.6% revenue CAGR and 36.9% net profit CAGR over 2026-28E.

Intact global demand for cleanroom technology, the edge AI market, 
and rapid cleanroom expansion in Asia further support Acter’s order 
backlog. Its backlog reached TWD66bn in July 2026 vs. TWD46bn in 2025 
and TWD30bn in 2022 (15.3% 2022-25 CAGR), which largely outpaced the 
global cleanroom technology market’s forecast CAGR over 2023-2032E, 
according to Precedence Research.

Strong competitive edge supports order share gains across multiple 
segments. Acter’s competitive edge lies in 1) intelligent management 
enabled by digital twins and AI, using 3D building information modelling 
(BIM) and AI-driven digital twin management; 2) intellectualisation of green 
engineering, which comprises green design, green procurement, and green 
techniques; 3) experience across multiple industries (ie, data centres and 
electromechanical engineering for the consumer industry) which supports 
better integration; and 4) a complete network, enabling on-the-spot support. 
Acter believes it has the highest market share among the operators of three 
major US data centre players due largely to its capabilities in liquid cooling 
and its expertise in electromechanical and cleanroom engineering.

Catalysts: Near-term: project wins from key clients, supporting stronger 
monthly revenue momentum; mid- to long-term: an improving operating-
margin trend and a resilient order backlog.

Valuation: We are the first non-domestic broker to cover Acter. We initiate 
coverage with a 12-month TP of TWD1,415, based on a target PER of 20x 
(equivalent to a 0.54x PEG over 2026-28E). We believe our valuation is 
fair, given Acter’s strong earnings visibility, which we believe warrants a 
rerating. Our TP is supported by a DCF valuation of TWD1,454.

Risks: 1) weaker-than-expected expansion in the semiconductor industry; 
and 2) project delays or cancellations.

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026

How do we justify our view? 
Growth outlook Valuation Earnings revisions

Growth outlook

Acter: net profit and net-profit growth    
We forecast Acter to record a 36.9% net profit CAGR over 
2026-28E, on the back of a 24.6% revenue CAGR over the 
same period, driven by 1) larger project scale, as strong 
demand for cleanroom EPCM services from the high-tech 
sector is likely to support continued growth in Acter’s order 
backlog and 2) improving gross margins and operating 
efficiency. We expect operating margin to expand by 2.0pp 
in 2027E and 1.3pp in 2028E, driven by favourable gross 
margins on new projects and operating leverage.

Source: Company, Daiwa forecasts

Valuation   Acter: 1-year-forward PER bands    
Our 12-month TP of TWD1,415 is based on a target PER 
of 20x, applied to our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. Our 
target multiple of 20x is higher than the stock’s past-3-year 
1-year-forward PER (12.2x) and past-5-year 1-year-
forward PER (9.6x) and implies a PEG of 0.54x on our 
36.9% net profit CAGR forecast over 2026-28E. We 
believe the stock deserves a rerating in the next 12 
months, largely supported by resilient cleanroom demand 
globally.

Our TP is also supported by an alternative DCF-derived 
valuation of TWD1,454/share.

Source: Bloomberg, Daiwa forecasts

Earnings revisions

Acter: Bloomberg consensus EPS forecast revisions   
Bloomberg consensus EPS estimates for 2026E and 
2027E have risen 24.8% and 24.3% YTD, respectively. 
This is likely due to upward revisions to order backlog and 
earnings forecasts following better-than-expected industry 
outlook. We are the first foreign broker to cover Acter.

Source: Bloomberg

Company profile 
Established in 1979, Acter Group (Acter) is a leading engineering services provider specialising in 
cleanroom, electromechanical, and high-tech facility integration solutions for semiconductor, 
optoelectronics, biotech, green energy, and advanced manufacturing clients across Taiwan, China and 
the rest of the world. As of 2025, its revenue comprised cleanroom electromechanical (71%), water, gas 
and chemical delivery systems (20%), consumer industry electromechanical (4%), biomedical (2%) and 
high-tech equipment & materials sales & services (3%).

### Page 5

5

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Acting on the demand for cleanrooms 
Why are cleanrooms a bottleneck in the high-tech 
industry? 
In recent years, cleanrooms have become a critical bottleneck for high-tech industries, 
especially semiconductor manufacturing, OSAT, AI hardware, and PCB, mainly because of 
its indispensable nature, severe supply-demand imbalance, labour shortages, and high 
technical and regulatory barriers.

Cleanrooms are an indispensable core infrastructure as they determine product 
yield and quality stability. Advanced manufacturing nodes in semiconductors, 
optoelectronics, and biopharmaceuticals require highly particle-free, constant-temperature, 
and constant-humidity environments to ensure reliable production. As process 
technologies continue to advance, such as 2nm node (or even newer nodes) and 
advanced packaging, existing cleanrooms must be rebuilt, and the complexity of building 
new ones thus increases. This keeps overall demand for cleanrooms at high levels.

A severe global supply-demand imbalance with demand for cleanroom construction 
outpacing supply. The surge in AI servers, high-performance computing (HPC), and high-
bandwidth memory (HBM) has triggered a global expansion for front-end wafer fabs, 
packaging and testing facilities, and PCB manufacturing. Order visibility remains extremely 
strong across the industry. According to Markets and Markets, the global cleanroom market 
size is set to expand at a CAGR of 6.6% over 2025-31E. At the same time, the global 
semiconductor cleanroom market size is set to grow at a CAGR of 8.0% over 2025-30E. 
For instance, Acter’s order backlog exceeded TWD66bn at the end of July 2026, up from 
TWD46bn at the end of 2025, showing the urgency of demand for cleanroom construction.

Global: cleanroom market size and growth      Global: semiconductor cleanroom market size and growth

Source: Markets and Markets  Source: Markets and Markets

Human resources are an additional limitation to capacity growth. Cleanroom 
engineering is highly technical and labour-intensive, relying on specialised on-site 
installation and supervision that cannot be replaced by robots. At the same time, the 
industry faces severe labour shortages and an aging workforce, and younger generations 
are reluctant to join field operations. Cross-border staff deployment is also less cost-
efficient than local hiring.

Cleanrooms involve complex system integration, creating high barriers to entry. A 
cleanroom construction includes 4 sequential phases, where each phase usually requires 
totally different areas of expertise. Therefore, the competitive landscape in each area 
differs. The process begins with civil & structural (C&S) works to establish the physical 
plant, followed by mechanical, electrical, and plumbing (MEP) works for essential utility 
generation, where Acter is one of the leading global experts. After MEP is finished, 
chemicals/gas/water are then distributed via main systems through primary piping 
network and finally delivered to production equipment through hook-up systems. As

### Page 6

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
global supply chains restructure under N+1 (domestic market + 1) strategies, companies 
building overseas facilities in places like the US face strict and complicated licensing 
processes, particularly on fire safety and hazardous chemicals. Without experience dealing 
with such engineering and safety issues, construction timelines can be severely delayed.

As a result, even with advanced equipment and chip designs, a high-tech company cannot 
begin production without adequate cleanroom space, skilled engineers, and system-
integration capabilities. Therefore, cleanroom engineering contractors hold significant 
bargaining power and capacity allocation leverage in today's tech supply chain.

IC foundry 
In the foundry sector, where the cleanroom supply-demand shortage is the tightest, project 
activity remains centred on advanced process capacity expansion in Taiwan and Arizona. 
Simultaneously, domestic advanced node development remains intense, with multi-phase 
fab builds advancing across Hsinchu, Taichung, and Kaohsiung. Local cleanroom and MEP 
integration demand is further boosted by advanced packaging facilities in Taiwan (such as 
Chiayi and Tainan) and upcoming projects in the US are set to contribute revenue for the 
cleanroom providers in the following years.

Memory 
The memory segment is exhibiting robust momentum as well, mainly catalysed by HBM 
capacity additions. Projects such as the plant in Japan, transitioned to contribution for the 
cleanroom EPCM providers starting from 2026. In Taiwan, turnkey engineering contracts in 
Miaoli are also actively underway. This trend is reinforced by stronger-than-expected 
expansion activity from memory producers in China.

OSAT,  PCB and ODM/OEM 
For OSAT companies, demand is also surging due to the spill-over effect of advanced 
packaging protocols and N+1 supply chain restructuring. The OSAT names are expanding 
their footprint both in Southern Taiwan and in overseas locations including in the US and 
Southeast Asia. Major engineering contracts signed between OSAT clients and Acter show 
stable long-term workload for strong cleanroom demand over our forecast horizon. 
Similarly, overseas expansion for PCB manufacturing is peaking in Southeast Asia, 
especially in Thailand. We expect Chinese PCB players to also increase their capacity, just 
like memory companies in the past year. Except for front-end and back-end chip 
manufacturing, ODM/OEM and data centre infrastructure is also a key growth engine for 
the EPCM industry. Major Taiwanese ODMs are building production facilities in Texas and 
Mexico to meet local manufacturing needs. Cleanroom contractors, such as Acter, 
providing MEP and system integration in Texas are directly benefiting from these projects. 
The shift toward liquid cooling systems has also increased engineering complexity, 
favouring few cleanroom companies with the expertise to deliver complex integration for 
CSPs in both North America and Southeast Asia.

Acter: an integrated cleanroom solutions provider  
Acter Group (Acter), an integrated cleanroom solutions provider, maintains its competitive 
positioning within the high-tech cleanroom and MEP engineering sector, driven by its group 
synergy and global footprint. Acter operates as a comprehensive vendor through a group 
business model. The parent company, Acter, supervises overall cleanroom and MEP 
services, while subsidiary Nova Technology (Novatech, 6613 TT, not rated) mainly provides 
chemical primary system and hook-up, and Novatech’s subsidiary Rayzher (7703 TT, not 
rated) provides gas primary system, hook-up and equipment. In China, Acter operates 
through Acter Technology Integration Group (603163 CH, not rated) and Suzhou Winmax 
(not listed) for Chinese EPCM (engineering, procurement, construction, and management) 
and equipment business. Except for C&S (civil and structural) works, where the margin is 
low (7-8%) and the contract duration is long, Acter’s services span nearly the entire 
cleanroom construction process. Its one-stop solutions model supports customer retention 
Acter provides

solutions via various 
subsidiaries

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
and improves bid-win rates, particularly for overseas expansion, where customers have 
few reliable providers to choose from.

Acter: organisational chart

Source: Company, Daiwa

To avoid the risk of over-concentration, Acter diversifies its client portfolio across 
semiconductor foundries, memory, PCB, OSAT, ODM hardware assembly, CSP data 
centres and the consumer industry. By sector, semiconductor projects represent the largest 
share of Acter’s portfolio, accounting for 66% of total revenue in 1H26, vs. 69% in 2025. 
Foundry manufacturing is the largest source of demand for cleanrooms in Taiwan, 
especially as advanced-node capacity expands at sites in Hsinchu, Taichung and 
Kaohsiung. In the memory segment, the company executes turnkey engineering builds to 
expand its capacity aggressively, creating cleanroom opportunities across Taiwan, Japan 
and Singapore. This multi-sector coverage decreases the risk of cyclical downturns in any 
individual segment, in our view.

By establishing 33 operating entities across 11 international regions, the company 
effectively captures capex migration driven by global N+1 supply chain diversification. This 
allows Acter to support Taiwanese and international clients as they expand manufacturing 
capacity from Taiwan into Southeast Asia, Japan and the US. Due to the complexity of 
advanced-node IC manufacturing, packaging and testing, it is especially important for 
these Taiwanese semiconductor companies to work with familiar cleanroom builders when 
expanding in the US. This is one of the reasons why Taiwanese cleanroom companies are 
receiving overseas orders and starting to set up global bases, in our view. Despite higher 
management, material, and manpower costs in the US, most cleanroom companies are 
benefitting from the trend by improving margins, given the higher complexity and risks to 
operate overseas. Across overseas hubs in Texas and Phoenix (expected to begin from 
2027), Acter prioritises local hiring and partners with regional civil contractors to shorten 
the learning curve and to avoid the high overhead. To further address the potential labour 
shortages in the future, the company has established cross-border academic and 
vocational partnerships to bring Southeast Asian talent to Taiwan for technical training 
before deploying them back to their home markets, securing future human capacity to 
support global expansion projects.

By product, cleanroom electromechanical services remain Acter’s largest source of 
revenue (accounting for 66% of 1H26 revenue, per the company). These services include 
cleanroom architectural and MEP engineering, which powers airflow, filtration, 
environmental regulation and utility controls. Water, gas and chemical delivery systems are 
its second-largest source of revenue (accounting for 24% of 1H26 revenue, per the 
company). Contributed mainly by Novatech (6613 TT, not rated) and Rayzher (7703 TT, not 
rated), these services cover the main systems and hook-up systems, for  ultra-pure water 
(UPW), specialty gases, chemical piping, etc., all of which require highly precise build-up 
work. Except for cleanroom electromechanical and water, gas and chemical delivery 
systems, the rest of Acter’s revenue comes from the consumer industry services 
electromechanical (5%), biomedical (3%), and high-tech equipment and materials sales 
and services (1%).

Acter: revenue by product

Source: Company, Daiwa

Technically, Acter integrates digital transformation and green engineering into its business 
model, helping it differentiate from peers. Acter deploys digital twin technology and 1:1 3D 
BIM modelling to detect piping interferences and optimise spatial layout before installation, 
minimising schedule delays. This makes Acter a more reliable option, in our view, and thus 
being a favourable supplier, especially for the semiconductor clients that have extremely 
high quality and swiftness criterion.

Bright outlook: strong order visibility 
Rising orders on hand due to supply-demand imbalance for cleanrooms 
Acter's order backlog has increased from TWD55bn in 1Q26 to TWD60bn in 2Q26 and 
TWD66bn in July 2026. We expect this figure to further increase in the following quarters, 
given the supply-demand imbalance in the cleanroom sector. This is composed of 
semiconductor (63%), electronic assembly (11%), consumer (9%) and others (17%), per 
the company. In 2H25, the company accelerated the digestion of small-scale projects 
below TWD1bn, and, as a result, starting from 1H26, it successfully shifted its project

Back-end packaging, cloud infrastructure and hardware assembly are additional growth 
drivers. Acter serves as a contractor for both Taiwanese and overseas OSAT providers, 
including TWD21.7bn contracts YTD, awarded by a major OSAT client vs. TWD16.6bn 
contract obtained from the same client in 2025. In cloud infrastructure, Acter maintains 
qualified supplier status with US CSPs, providing mechanical, electrical and plumbing 
(MEP) integration, especially for those data centres that implement liquid-cooling systems. 
The company also wins factory construction projects for Taiwanese ODMs across the US 
and Southeast Asia, as well as PCB facility projects in Thailand and China. Non-tech 
sectors, including biotechnology, commercial buildings, smart logistics hubs and medical 
facilities, comprise 9% of the total backlog, per management.

Competitive advantages 
High entry barriers to the segment 
Comprehensive product portfolio, empowered by subsidiaries 
Acter provides a one-stop EPCM solution, leveraging its subsidiaries to deliver complete 
solutions. Within the group, the parent company Acter focuses on overall cleanroom and 
MEP system integration; subsidiary Novatech provides primary chemical systems and 
hook-up systems; and sub-subsidiary Rayzer specialises in primary specialty-gas systems 
and hook-up systems. In China, Suzhou Winmax, also a subsidiary of Novatech, focuses 
on the supply of wet processing and chemical handling equipment. This integrated model 
covers cleanroom engineering integration, water, power, chemical and gas-utility systems, 
as well as post-completion validation and maintenance, ensuring cross-system 
compatibility while mitigating risks associated with multi-contractor hiring.

Under global supply chain diversification trends, this unified group strategy strengthens 
order-winning capabilities in overseas markets. Clients expanding internationally face 
regulatory, linguistic and supply-chain complexities, favouring partners that offer one-stop 
services with a proven cross-border track record.

A diverse client base helps Acter weather economic cycles 
Acter’s main competitive advantage is its diversification of clients by industry and region. 
This guides the company through the peaks and troughs of the economy, which is 
especially crucial given the strong cyclicality of the semiconductor/high-tech industry.  Acter 
serves a broad client base across semiconductors, OSAT, PCB, electronics assembly 
(ODM), CSP data centres, biopharmaceuticals and even the consumer industry (including 
commercial real estate and logistics), whereas many peers rely on a few large foundry 
clients. When semiconductor demand slows, capacity expansion in the PCB or data centre 
sectors offset the decline. Additionally, Acter avoids concentration risk and margin 
compression from major foundry accounts, as projects from leading foundries yield lower 
margins; by serving niche markets like US-based CSPs, the company achieves higher 
gross margins.

Geographically, Acter operates across Taiwan, China, Southeast Asia, the US and other 
countries. The company has OSAT and foundry customers in Taiwan and China, PCB 
clients expanding into Thailand, and ODM and data centre clients establishing operations 
in the US. Across different countries, local hiring and local supply chain implementation 
can further reduce costs and increase efficiency relative to peers reliant on a cross-border 
labour force.

Labour shortages 
Amid global labour shortages, Acter minimises the operational impact and turns workforce 
constraints into a moat through localised talent development. Since engineering labour 
cannot easily be automated, Acter has built a cross-border talent pipeline via industry-
academia partnerships. The company recruits Southeast Asian students for specialised 
M&E engineering training in Taiwan, deploying them to global project sites after their 
graduation to solve labour bottlenecks. This structure maintains an engineering team with 
an average tenure of more than 7 years, creating an operational barrier in an industry with 
a high employee turnover rate.

Compared with peers relying on high-cost personnel sent from Taiwan, we note that Acter’s 
local hiring strategy creates cost and efficiency advantages. Prioritising compliant local 
hiring across overseas markets reduces travel expenses while accelerating integration with 
local supply chains and regulations. Localised teams also handle post-completion services 
and technical support directly on-site, building long-term client trust in overseas markets.

The supply of cleanroom 
services is limited due to 
high entry barriers in the 
segment

### Page 11

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Numerous competitors, each with different specialisation 
Acter faces different competitors across its main product lines. In the EPCM segment, 
including cleanroom and MEP works, key competitors include UIS (2404 TT, not rated), 
L&K (6139 TT, not rated), Yankey (6691 TT, not rated) and Jiuhan (6903 TT, not rated). 
Despite having business models similar to Acter, UIS, Yankey and Jiuhan focus more on 
major semiconductor players in both Taiwan and the US, and L&K concentrates on ASEAN 
semiconductor projects. In the equipment/material sales and agency space, Acter mainly 
competes with Marketech (6196 TT, not rated). In the water, gas and chemical delivery 
systems area, through Novatech and Rayzher, Acter group competes with Marketech, 
Mega Union (6944 TT, not rated), Taiwan Puritic (6826 TT, not rated), Trusval (6667 TT, not 
rated) and Cica-Huntek (6725 TT, not rated).

In our view, despite the competitive environment, Acter maintains a formidable competitive 
moat built on several key advantages. We attribute this to its more than 45 year experience 
in the industry, enabling it to develop specialised expertise that is hard for competitors to 
replicate.

Financial analysis 
We forecast the company’s net profit to rise at a CAGR of 36.9% over 2026-28E, driven by 
a revenue CAGR of 24.6% due to surging demand from the high-tech industry (an increase 
in the order backlog to TWD66bn at the end of July 2026 vs. TWD46bn at end-2025) and a 
3.3pp expansion in operating margin over 2026-28E, driven by the gross margin 
improvement from more favourable project wins and operating leverage.

Strong order visibility, with revenue expected to grow 
across all sectors over 2026-28E 
We forecast Acter’s revenue to increase at a CAGR of 24.6% over 2026-28E, driven by 
scale expansion and a growing demand from various industries, including semiconductor, 
electronic assembly, optoelectronics and others.

Acter: revenue growth trend

Source: Company, Daiwa forecasts

Gross- and operating-margin expansion trend  
Over 2026-28E, we forecast Acter’s gross and operating margins to expand on rising 
revenue scale and a higher complexity of cleanrooms for advanced manufacturing. We 
note that the gross margin from high-tech cleanrooms is higher than that from consumer 
industries, such as commercial buildings and biopharmaceuticals. Thus, we estimate the 
company’s gross margin to reach 26.0% in 2028E from 18.9% in 2025 and its operating 
margin to reach 21.6% in 2028E from 13.7% in 2025.

Acter: gross- and operating-margin trend

Source: Company, Daiwa forecasts

(from TWD6,325m in 2026E). We expect its net margin to come in at 13.1% in 2027E and 
14.2% in 2028E (vs. 11.7% in 2026E) and for its net profit to grow at a CAGR of 36.9% 
over 2026-28E.
